```
{"id":"ABA-393","title":"Orchestrator-enforced completion: finishing sub-agent recovers stranded committed work","description":"## What\n\nAs the drain-cycle operator running an unattended cycle, I want the orchestrator to drive a committed-but-unfinished issue to completion through a fresh finishing sub-agent before it halts, so that a weak worker that commits its slices but skips the finishing protocol no longer strands reviewable work or stops the run.\n\nThe seam lives in `_drain_one_issue` (`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`), at the two terminal halt sites: the not-Done halt (`:781`, slug `err-issue-not-done`) and the stack-no-PRs halt (`:706`, slug `err-stack-no-prs`). Before either reverts and records, the orchestrator inspects the worktree branch. With commits beyond base (`git rev-list <base>..HEAD --count > 0`) and the issue not properly closed, it spawns a finishing-only sub-agent — reusing `worker.run_issue` with a new `prompt.build_finishing` (alongside `_stack_preamble` / `_resume_directive`) — then re-reads `linear.get_issue` and `handoff.read`. On success it joins the existing happy path (`:733`): extend the baton, record submitted PRs, tear down. On failure it falls through to the unchanged halt, now naming that recovery was attempted.\n\n**Incident that motivated this:** in cycle `793fc9ea`, <issue id=\"96cabf59-da7d-4eb7-9032-7ea75663163a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-383/n08-reference-sweep-fresh-install-check\">ABA-383</issue> ran on a `model:haiku` worker that committed 4 reviewable slices but exited without running review → `/shape:pr-finishing` → handoff → Done. The not-Done halt reverted the issue and stranded the branch.\n\n## Why\n\nCompleteness is the orchestrator's contract to enforce, not the worker's to be trusted with. The orchestrator already reads the proof signals (`final_linear_state`, `handoff.read`); the only missing move is to act on a recoverable gap instead of halting on it. The finishing sequence is mechanical and was proven by hand on <issue id=\"96cabf59-da7d-4eb7-9032-7ea75663163a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-383/n08-reference-sweep-fresh-install-check\">ABA-383</issue>, so a fixed **sonnet** sub-agent runs it reliably regardless of the issue's `model:` label, while any Critical/Required code fix — the part that needs capability — delegates to **opus**.\n\n**Rejected:** orchestrator runs `gh`/`gt` and posts the Linear comment itself — moves submission logic back into the orchestrator that AGENTS.md deliberately stripped it from. **Rejected:** trust uncommitted working-tree changes — completeness of an uncommitted tree is unknowable, so an empty or uncommitted-only branch stays a genuine failure and halts as today.\n\n## Completion\n\n* Done when: on the not-Done path, an issue left with commits beyond base and state ≠ Done triggers a sonnet finishing sub-agent; a post-spawn re-check reading Done continues the run, and still-not-Done halts with a reason naming the attempted recovery.\n* Done when: on the stack-no-PRs path, an issue Done with commits beyond base but no `pr_urls` triggers the same finishing sub-agent; a re-check reading non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton, and still-empty `pr_urls` halts.\n* Done when: an issue whose branch has no commits beyond base, or whose only changes are uncommitted, halts with the existing reason and spawns no finishing sub-agent.\n* Done when: the finishing sub-agent runs on `claude-sonnet-4-6` and its prompt instructs it to delegate Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`; at most one finishing attempt runs per issue per run; and a finishing spawn is recorded as its own run-log entry.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] **skeleton** — On the not-Done halt path in `_drain_one_issue`, detect commits beyond base, add `prompt.build_finishing`, spawn a sonnet finishing sub-agent via `worker.run_issue`, and re-check `linear.get_issue` + `handoff.read`, recovering to the happy path on Done or falling through to the existing halt (real nested `claude -p` spawn, not a stub). Done when: an issue exited not-Done with commits beyond base runs the finishing sub-agent and either reaches Done-and-continues or halts with a recovery-attempted reason, exercised end-to-end. *(Model: Opus)*\n- [ ] Extend the same recovery to the stack-no-PRs halt path (`err-stack-no-prs`): an issue Done with commits beyond base but empty `pr_urls` runs the finishing sub-agent and a re-check with non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton. Done when: a Done-but-no-`pr_urls` issue with commits beyond base is recovered to non-empty `pr_urls` and extends the baton, or halts unchanged when recovery fails. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n- [ ] Harden and bound the recovery — run the finishing sub-agent on `claude-sonnet-4-6` with a prompt that delegates Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`, guard one finishing attempt per issue per run, write a run-log entry for the finishing spawn, and enrich the fall-through halt reason. Done when: a second finishing attempt within one run is refused, the finishing spawn appears as its own run-log entry, and the prompt pins sonnet with an opus fix-delegation instruction. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* **Finishing sub-agent loops / re-strands work.** Mitigation: one-attempt-per-issue-per-run guard; `max_resume_attempts` is the cross-run backstop.\n* **Finishing sub-agent re-implements rather than finishes.** Mitigation: `build_finishing` prompt states the work is already committed and forbids re-implementation. Falsifier: a finishing run whose post-spawn `<base>..HEAD` diff adds non-review commits.\n* **\"Committed beyond base\" misfires on an ambiguous base.** Mitigation: resolve `<base>` from the same value the worker prompt used and assert it is an ancestor of HEAD before spawning.\n\n---\n\n## Relationship to this project (reconciliation — read before build)\n\nThis ticket is the **orchestrator-side backstop** for the same goal the six in-worker roles serve: completeness enforced, not trusted. It must be designed jointly with three siblings:\n\n* <issue id=\"080d520d-331a-41c8-888f-af2425d81e7a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-328/halt-on-fail-verify-flow-ticket-failing-outcome-verifier-halts-cycle\">ABA-328</issue> **(Halt-on-fail) — MANDATORY exclusion.** <issue id=\"080d520d-331a-41c8-888f-af2425d81e7a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-328/halt-on-fail-verify-flow-ticket-failing-outcome-verifier-halts-cycle\">ABA-328</issue> makes an Outcome-Verifier FAIL *stay halted*; this ticket makes a not-Done/no-PR exit *auto-recover*. Both rewrite the same halt decision in `_drain_one_issue`. The recovery here must fire **only on \"committed-but-unfinished\", never on \"committed-but-verifier-rejected\"** — otherwise it would submit and Done a ticket the verifier deliberately rejected, breaking **KR2 (zero silent Dones)**. Detection must read the verifier verdict and skip recovery on FAIL.\n* <issue id=\"5e888b35-f824-4b90-b5d3-7b4c31c21237\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-330/worker-invokes-pr-preparer-after-outcome-verifier-pass-prep-verdict\">ABA-330</issue> **(PR Preparer) — KR3 alignment.** The finishing agent submits via `/shape:pr-finishing`; KR3 requires every PR carry a structured What/Why/Focus body or auto-merge route via `/shape:pr-prepare`. The finishing prompt must chain pr-prepare → pr-finishing so recovered PRs satisfy KR3.\n* <issue id=\"888a8054-6434-4c02-b2b6-861f60e05706\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-321/run-log-schema-outcome-verdict-prep-verdict-responder-runs-present\">ABA-321</issue> **(run-log schema) — recording alignment.** Record the finishing spawn in the standardized schema (a `finishing_runs[]` field shaped like `responder_runs[]`), so `drain-cycle grade` can audit recovered Dones uniformly.\n\n**Scope note:** this recovery is **not** verify-flow-gated today — it applies to every stack-mode drain. Decide at build whether it stays universal (a general safety net) or is gated to the verify flow to match the project's other roles.\n\nDelivery plan: `docs/delivery-plans/orchestrator-enforced-completion/` (passes `walk-delivery-plan` + `check-plan-framing`). Relates to the stacked-PR submission work (the same halt-and-chain machinery).","projectMilestone":{"id":"36f3d280-d50c-49ed-a7f6-57e2f11e0e7a","name":"3 — Gate Done on the outcome verdict"},"priority":{"value":2,"name":"High"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-393/orchestrator-enforced-completion-finishing-sub-agent-recovers-stranded","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-393-orchestrator-enforced-completion-finishing-sub-agent","createdAt":"2026-06-15T02:28:12.222Z","updatedAt":"2026-06-15T02:33:43.449Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Backlog","statusType":"backlog","labels":[],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"stateHistory":[{"state":{"id":"547299fd-73d7-4f66-994b-e4d7b30caac8","name":"Backlog","type":"backlog"},"startedAt":"2026-06-15T02:28:12.222Z","endedAt":null}],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Multi-agent collaboration for correctness","projectId":"812bc9b5-1618-48b8-8041-6d6223dc6d4c","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1","relations":{"blocks":[],"blockedBy":[],"relatedTo":[{"id":"ABA-321","title":"Run-log schema: `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, `responder_runs[]` present (null) on every drain"},{"id":"ABA-330","title":"Worker invokes PR Preparer after Outcome Verifier pass; `prep_verdict` written to run log"},{"id":"ABA-328","title":"Halt-on-fail: `verify`-flow ticket failing Outcome Verifier halts cycle with worktree intact"},{"id":"ABA-383","title":"N08 — Reference sweep + fresh-install check"}],"duplicateOf":null}}
```

```
{"id":"ABA-370","title":"N01 — Thin-supervisor contract design doc (before build)","description":"`design-doc` · serves **KR3** · **Rule A1 branch** (D1 trips one trigger: one-way-door contract on shared infrastructure across two repos) · blocks N02–N04 · blocked by A/N04 (<issue id=\"6440b109-6e23-43b2-b176-38cc144bb14c\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-361/n04-execution-workflow-design-doc-before-build\">ABA-361</issue>) — this doc *extends* A's inter-skill handoff contract to the supervisor seam; it does not re-decide the skill graph. The initiative's flagged Frontier exception.\n\n**Delegates to:** design-doc skill (Rule A1 design doc — produced before the build nodes are picked up).\n**On pickup — before any build node in this plan:** produce the design doc via the `design-doc` skill and accept it through the `plan-review` exit gate. N02–N04's task breakdown waits until this is accepted.\n\n## What\n\nThe design doc for the supervisor↔worker contract: which segments the ≤15-line prompt template carries (issue context, worktree, base branch, skill pointer — whether the resume directive counts as process context or moves, and **how the per-issue stack/push policy reaches the worker**: `stack` is a supervisor-resolved fact (`--no-stack`, `push_to_main_repos`, `orchestrator.py:505`) that today selects the preamble variant, so the pointer-only template must still transmit it as a process segment); the `.drain-handoff.json` schema v2 — the verdict fields (`outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, `flow`, halt reason on non-Done exits), who writes each and who reads each; the boundary chart allocating every current prompt/orchestrator concern to *process* (supervisor keeps: spawn, guardrails, halt, resume, grade) or *workflow* (pack owns: everything else, including verify-flow routing); and the vendor-agnostic prose constraints that let a non-Claude worker follow the same pointer.\n\n## Why\n\nOne accepted contract lets the prompt re-scope and both verdict halves build against a stable seam instead of three locally-sensible interfaces negotiated through PRs. Rejected alternative: re-scope the prompt ad hoc and let the handoff schema grow fields as needs appear — schema drift across two repos with no single source, exactly the two-sources-of-truth failure the thin-supervisor architecture exists to end. Unblocks N02–N04; gives any future non-Claude worker a written contract to be tested against.\n\n## Completion\n\nAn accepted design doc covering: the prompt-segment allocation with the ≤15-line budget spent line by line — including the per-issue stack/push-policy signal and the grep pattern that defines \"procedure verb\" for KR3's check; the handoff schema v2 with writer/reader allocation per field; the process-vs-workflow boundary chart (including where `flow.py`'s verify-label routing and `prompt.py`'s shape-task directive go); and the vendor-agnostic constraints. Accepted through the `plan-review` gate.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* Initiative A's design doc (A/N04, <issue id=\"6440b109-6e23-43b2-b176-38cc144bb14c\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-361/n04-execution-workflow-design-doc-before-build\">ABA-361</issue>) is accepted before this one is drafted — this doc extends its handoff contract rather than competing with it *(to-verify — if it slips, this node inherits its namespace and contract questions and grows accordingly)*\n* The ≤15-line cap is measured on the worker prompt template, with process segments counted inside the budget *(verified — KR3's own measurement clause:* `wc -l` *on the template plus grep for procedure verbs)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* **Risk:** this contract drifts from A/N04's inter-skill handoff contract — two documents describing one file's schema. *Mitigation:* schema v2 amends A/N04's handoff table in place (one table, both initiatives cite it); this doc adds the supervisor-seam columns rather than copying the table.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] Frame the constraints and draft ≥2 alternatives for the prompt-segment allocation and handoff schema v2 · Done when: the doc presents the alternatives with trade-offs against the ≤15-line cap and vendor portability · Model: Frontier · risk one-way · review elevated · axes RC·SC·HS·SR·OR = H·H·L·H·L · companions code-review-and-quality\n- [ ] Record the decision (boundary chart + schema v2 + segment allocation) and pass the plan-review exit gate · Done when: the design doc is accepted and N02–N04 can break tasks against it · Model: Frontier · risk one-way · review elevated · axes H·H·L·H·L · companions code-review-and-quality\n\n---\n\nPlan source: {"id":"ABA-371","title":"N02 — Pointer-only prompt template","description":"`story` · serves **KR3** · **skeleton** (the initiative's walking skeleton — the thinnest supervisor that still drains an issue end-to-end) · lands in the **drain-cycle** repo · blocked by N01 (task breakdown waits for the accepted contract), A/N05 (<issue id=\"4c68570a-7b0f-4e76-b950-4bd705f26046\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-363/n05-entry-skill-the-front-door\">ABA-363</issue> — an entry skill must exist) and A/N10 (<issue id=\"90eb9224-9068-4b91-bd61-73d35697611f\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-367/n10-drain-cycle-prompt-pointer\">ABA-367</issue> — the pointer swap lands first; this node deletes the machinery around the pointer, so the branches must chain in that order).\n\n**Delegates to:** at-pickup task breakdown (story). The diff is subtractive — collapse, don't rewrite.\n\n## What\n\nAs the drain-cycle supervisor, I want my worker prompt reduced to issue context plus process segments plus a single skill pointer, so that the intra-issue workflow lives only in the pack and any vendor's worker can follow it unchanged. Concretely: `_normal_preamble`, `_stack_preamble`, `_TAIL`, and `_STACK_TAIL` in `drain_cycle/prompt.py` collapse into one ≤15-line template, and workflow routing the supervisor still carries (the shape-task directive; `flow.py`'s verify-label branch) moves or dies per N01's boundary chart.\n\n## Why\n\nSubtraction is the whole re-scope — KR3 is a brake measured by `wc -l`, grep, and pytest, and every line that leaves the prompt is a line that can never again drift from the pack's prose. Rejected alternative: keep the inlined procedure behind a fallback flag next to the pointer — two sources of truth, and workers follow the stale one (the same rejection A/N10 recorded for its scope). Unblocks N05's validation cycles and opens the non-Claude-worker door.\n\n## Completion\n\n* **Done when:** `wc -l` on the emitted worker prompt template is ≤15 and grep for procedure-verb sequences (review/fix/commit/push) over `drain_cycle/` returns empty.\n* **Done when:** pytest exits 0 with the test suite updated to the new template.\n* **Done when:** one smoke drain on a low-stakes issue reaches PR through the new template with no inlined-procedure fallback observed in the transcript.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* A/N10's pointer swap has landed before pickup — this node edits the same file and assumes the third-party reference is already gone *(to-verify — checked at pickup via the blockedBy edge; if N10 slipped, this node halts rather than absorbing its scope)*\n* The pack's PR-finishing skill (A/N08) carries the handoff-write and review-trail steps, so the prompt can drop them *(to-verify — checked against the accepted A/N04 contract at pickup; a gap re-opens N01's boundary chart, not this node's scope)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* **Risk:** the ≤15-line budget proves too tight for genuine process context (resume directive, stack signal, worktree facts) and the cap gets quietly exceeded. *Mitigation:* N01 allocates the budget line by line before this node breaks tasks; if a process segment doesn't fit, the KR cap is challenged at plan-review — never silently blown.\n* **Risk:** dropping the inlined procedure degrades worker behaviour mid-cycle. *Mitigation:* the kill condition is the brake — 3 consecutive halted drains the inlined prompt would have completed restores the inlined tail; the collapse is one commit, a two-way door.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] `skeleton` — Collapse the preamble/tail variants in `prompt.py` into the ≤15-line pointer template per the accepted contract (test updates folded in) · Done when: `wc -l` on the emitted template is ≤15 and pytest is green · Model: Fast · axes RC·SC·HS·SR·OR = M·L·L·M·L\n- [ ] Remove or relocate the supervisor's workflow routing (shape-task directive, verify-label branch) per N01's boundary chart · Done when: grep for procedure-verb sequences over `drain_cycle/` returns empty and pytest is green · Model: Fast · axes M·L·L·M·L\n- [ ] Smoke-drain one low-stakes issue through the new template · Done when: the drain reaches PR with no inlined-procedure fallback observed in the transcript · Model: Fast · axes M·M·L·L·L\n\n---\n\nPlan source: [N02](<https://github.com/ababushkin/agent-skills-shaper/blob/main/examples/delivery-plans/drain-cycle-thin-supervisor/D1-supervisor-sheds-workflow-prose/N02-pointer-only-prompt-template.md>)","projectMilestone":{"id":"7795d606-90a7-443c-b390-d322ef8dea3a","name":"5 {"id":"ABA-372","title":"N03 — Pack skills emit verdicts into the handoff","description":"`story` · serves **KR2** · lands in the **agent-skills-shaper** repo (extends the execution skills initiative A authors) · blocked by N01 (task breakdown waits for the accepted handoff schema v2) and A's D2 skill stack (<issue id=\"4c68570a-7b0f-4e76-b950-4bd705f26046\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-363/n05-entry-skill-the-front-door\">ABA-363</issue>–366 — this node edits those skills' verify and PR-finishing steps, so it cannot start before they exist).\n\n**Delegates to:** at-pickup task breakdown (story). The diff extends A's skills at their handoff seams — it does not add new skills.\n\n## What\n\nAs the operator inspecting a drain, I want the pack's verify and PR-preparation steps to write their verdicts into the handoff file, so that the supervisor can record them without owning any workflow knowledge. The handoff file already carries `pr_title`/`pr_body`/`findings`; this node adds the schema-v2 verdict fields (`outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, `flow`) at the workflow steps that produce them, plus the halt reason on blocked/non-Done exits.\n\n## Why\n\nThe handoff file is already the proven worker→supervisor channel, so verdicts ride the existing seam with zero new transport. Rejected alternative: the supervisor parses worker transcripts for verdicts — couples inspection to one vendor's transcript format, which is exactly the coupling this initiative exists to remove. Unblocks N04's read side; the write-half of KR2.\n\n## Completion\n\n* **Done when:** a toy-issue dry run through the execution skills leaves a handoff file with `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, and `flow` populated per schema v2.\n* **Done when:** a blocked-issue dry run leaves a handoff naming the halt reason while the issue stays In Progress per workflow governance.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* A's execution skills have distinct verify and PR-preparation steps where verdict emission can attach *(verified — A/N05's front door names verify and PR as named delegations, and A/N08 owns the handoff write; the seams exist by contract)*\n* Verdict emission is a light extension of those steps, not a redesign *(to-verify — if a step produces no structured verdict object at all, this node adds the object at the skill seam; the supervisor side is unaffected either way)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* **Risk:** the fields the pack writes drift from the fields the supervisor reads — two repos, one schema. *Mitigation:* schema v2 lives in one table (N01's contract, amending A/N04's); N04's tests parse a fixture handoff written verbatim from that table, so drift fails a test before it fails a drain.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] `skeleton` — Add verdict emission to the verify and PR-finishing steps per schema v2 (fixture handoff example folded in) · Done when: a toy-issue dry run leaves a handoff with outcome_verdict, prep_verdict, and flow populated per schema v2 · Model: Fast · axes RC·SC·HS·SR·OR = M·L·L·L·L\n- [ ] Write the blocked/halt emission path · Done when: a blocked-issue dry run leaves a handoff naming the halt reason and the issue status is unchanged · Model: Fast · axes M·M·L·L·L\n\n---\n\nPlan source: [N03](<https://github.com/ababushkin/agent-skills-shaper/blob/main/examples/delivery-plans/drain-cycle-thin-supervisor/D2-every-drain-inspectable/N03-pack-skills-emit-verdicts.md>)","projectMilestone":{"id":"a668c8fa-f026-4247-b3c5-c491c9865642","name":"2 {"id":"ABA-373","title":"N04 — Supervisor records verdicts on every exit","description":"`story` · serves **KR2** · lands in the **drain-cycle** repo · blocked by N01 (task breakdown waits for the accepted handoff schema v2). Buildable in parallel with N03: the read side tests against fixture handoffs written from the schema table, not against N03's live output.\n\n**Delegates to:** at-pickup task breakdown (story).\n\n## What\n\nAs the operator grading cycles, I want every worker exit — Done, halted, errored — to land a run-log entry with verdicts and halt_reason, so that no drain is uninspectable. The run-log schema already has the fields; nothing populates them: `outcome_verdict` and `prep_verdict` are initialised to `None` at `orchestrator.py:647-649` and never assigned. This node has `handoff.py` parse the schema-v2 verdict fields and the orchestrator land them on every `append_entry` path.\n\n## Why\n\nReading verdicts from the handoff closes KR2 with a bounded diff on a file the orchestrator already reads at every Done exit — no new I/O, no new failure mode. Rejected alternative: the supervisor re-derives verdicts by inspecting PRs and Linear comments — re-creates workflow knowledge inside the supervisor, violating the boundary this initiative draws. Unblocks N05's grading (the schema check is one of its three measurements).\n\n## Completion\n\n* **Done when:** a stubbed Done-path drain writes a run-log entry with `flow`, `outcome_verdict`, and `prep_verdict` populated from the handoff, pytest green.\n* **Done when:** a forced-halt test writes an entry carrying `halt_reason` plus whatever verdicts the partial handoff held.\n* **Done when:** the KR2 schema check exits 0 over a compliant run log and non-zero when a Done entry lacks verdicts.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* Every exit path flows through `runlog.append_entry`, so populating at that seam covers Done, halted, and errored exits alike *(verified — Done and halt paths both call* `log.append_entry` *in [orchestrator.py](<http://orchestrator.py>); the run-log docstring documents the halt-entry contract)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* **Risk:** a worker that crashes before writing the handoff leaves verdicts null, and the schema check reads the drain as a KR2 failure when it is actually inspectable via halt_reason. *Mitigation:* the schema check encodes \"inspectable\", not \"non-null\": null verdicts are compliant when `halt_reason` (or a non-Done exit state) explains them; a null on a Done entry is the violation.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] `skeleton` — Parse schema-v2 verdict fields in `handoff.py` and populate Done-path run-log entries (fixture-handoff test folded in) · Done when: a stubbed drain writes an entry with flow, outcome_verdict, and prep_verdict populated and pytest is green · Model: Fast · axes RC·SC·HS·SR·OR = M·L·L·L·L\n- [ ] Populate halt/error exits with halt_reason plus any partial-handoff verdicts · Done when: a forced-halt test writes an entry where halt_reason is non-null and available verdicts are carried · Model: Fast · axes M·L·L·L·L\n- [ ] Add the KR2 schema check (one-liner or small script beside the run logs) · Done when: the check exits 0 over a compliant run log and non-zero when a Done entry lacks verdicts · Model: Fast · axes L·L·L·L·L\n\n---\n\nPlan source: [N04](<https://github.com/ababushkin/agent-skills-shaper/blob/main/examples/delivery-plans/drain-cycle-thin-supervisor/D2-every-drain-inspectable/N04-supervisor-records-verdicts.md>)","projectMilestone":{"id":"a668c8fa-f026-4247-b3c5-c491c9865642","name":"2 — Record the pack's verdicts in the run log"},"priority":{"value":3,"name":"Medium"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-373/n04-supervisor-records-verdicts-on-every-exit","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-373-n04-supervisor-records-verdicts-on-every-exit","createdAt":"2026-06-10T12:48:59.668Z","updatedAt":"2026-06-15T13:28:39.556Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Backlog","statusType":"backlog","labels":["haiku","drain-cycle"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"stateHistory":[{"state":{"id":"547299fd-73d7-4f66-994b-e4d7b30caac8","name":"Backlog","type":"backlog"},"startedAt":"2026-06-10T12:48:59.668Z","endedAt":null}],"createdBy":"Anton {"id":"ABA-374","title":"N05 — Validation: 2 cycles on the pointer prompt","description":"`experiment` · serves **KR1** · **acceptance: verifies D3** — close LAST so the milestone hitting 100% coincides with the KR observation · blocked by N02–N04 (everything under test must have landed) and A/N09 (<issue id=\"bfcdbf63-3a34-40b4-a50d-6aa30fe539e6\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-368/n09-validation-drains-5-issues-45\">ABA-368</issue> — the execution skills should be proven on live drains before the supervisor is thinned, so a failure here indicts the re-scope, not the skills).\n\n**Delegates to:** product-spike (experiment discipline — hypothesis, success metric, written finding).\n**On pickup:** run the experiment and write the finding (confirmed / falsified). Not a build node: honest outcomes are *confirmed* or *falsified*, not *shipped*. Because the measurement window is shared, this node also runs the KR2 schema check over the same logs.\n\n## What\n\nDrain the first 2 real cycles after the re-scope lands with the pointer-only prompt, and grade the bet: did ≥1 cycle complete with zero manual fix commits? Each cycle's evidence is three measurements — `drain-cycle grade` over the run logs, the prompt-template diff (the ≤15-line template in force), and the KR2 schema check (every exit inspectable). Watch the kill condition throughout. Write the finding.\n\n## Why\n\nKR1 is the stretch bet, and only live cycles can grade it — N02's smoke drain proves one issue flows, not that a whole cycle's mess (halts, resumes, stacked PRs, mixed repos) survives a supervisor that no longer says how to work. Rejected alternative: declare victory on the smoke drain — the unverified-claim failure this pack exists to prevent. `acceptance` because D3's Done is irreducible to its children: there is no build node whose completion implies a cycle drains.\n\n## Completion\n\n**Hypothesis:** a full cycle drains to completion with the pointer-only prompt at the execution initiative's quality bar (no manual fix commits), with every worker exit inspectable.\n\n**Success metric:** `drain-cycle grade` + prompt-template diff + KR2 schema check over the first 2 cycles after the re-scope lands = **≥1 clean full-cycle drain**.\n\nZero clean drains in the window **falsifies** the bet — the re-scope is re-shaped, not extended. Three consecutive halted drains that the inlined prompt would have completed fires the initiative **kill condition**: restore the inlined tail; keep the re-scope to guardrails and logging only.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* Two cycles of ordinary work are available in the measurement window *(verified — the KR's own \"measured over\" clause anticipates this window, and the backlog convention keeps a cycle stream)*\n* \"Drains the inlined prompt would have completed\" is judgeable from halt transcripts *(to-verify — the kill condition needs a per-halt judgement note; if a halt is ambiguous, it does not count toward the 3)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* **Risk:** the 2 cycles are dominated by markdown-pack work and a clean drain overstates the re-scope's generality. *Mitigation:* include at least one drain-cycle (Python) issue across the window so both repo types exercise the pointer prompt.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] `skeleton` — Drain the first post-re-scope cycle; record grade, template line count, and schema-check result in a trial note · Done when: the run log and trial note for cycle 1 exist with all three measurements · Model: Fast · axes RC·SC·HS·SR·OR = M·M·L·L·L\n- [ ] `acceptance` — Drain the second cycle; evaluate the ≥1-clean-cycle metric and the kill condition; write the finding (confirmed / falsified) — this finding is D3's Done condition · Done when: the finding is written with per-cycle evidence links · Model: Balanced · axes H·M·L·L·L\n\n---\n\nPlan source: [N05](<https://github.com/ababushkin/agent-skills-shaper/blob/main/examples/delivery-plans/drain-cycle-thin-supervisor/D3-full-cycle-on-pointer-prompt/N05-validation-cycle-drains.md>)","projectMilestone":{"id":"96358131-30f3-4cf1-a7fc-8bc90e1da590","name":"4 — Grade the outcome"},"priority":{"value":3,"name":"Medium"},"url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-374/n05-validation-2-cycles-on-the-pointer-prompt","gitBranchName":"anton/aba-374-n05-validation-2-cycles-on-the-pointer-prompt","createdAt":"2026-06-10T12:49:25.532Z","updatedAt":"2026-06-15T13:28:25.340Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"startedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"dueDate":null,"slaStartedAt":null,"slaMediumRiskAt":null,"slaHighRiskAt":null,"slaBreachesAt":null,"slaType":"all","status":"Backlog","statusType":"backlog","labels":["acceptance","product-spike","sonnet","agent-skills-shaper"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"stateHistory":[{"state":{"id":"547299fd-73d7-4f66-994b-e4d7b30caac8","name":"Backlog","type":"backlog"},"startedAt":"2026-06-10T12:49:25.532Z","endedAt":null}],"createdBy":"Anton Babushkin","createdById":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","project":"Multi-agent collaboration for correctness","projectId":"812bc9b5-1618-48b8-8041-6d6223dc6d4c","team":"Personal","teamId":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1","relations":{"blocks":[],"blockedBy":[{"id":"ABA-368","title":"N09 — Validation drains (5 issues, ≥4/5)"},{"id":"ABA-372","title":"N03 — Pack skills emit verdicts into the handoff"},{"id":"ABA-371","title":"N02 — Pointer-only prompt template"},{"id":"ABA-373","title":"N04 — Supervisor records verdicts on every exit"}],"relatedTo":[],"duplicateOf":null}}
```

```
{"id":"ABA-321","title":"Run-log schema: `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, `responder_runs[]` present (null) on every drain","description":"## What\n\nThree new fields are present on every run-log entry: `outcome_verdict` (null), `prep_verdict` (null), `responder_runs` (empty array). Their shapes are documented in-code.\n\n## Why\n\nThese are the write targets for the Outcome Verifier (M2), PR Preparer (M3), and PR Responder (M4). They must exist before any role can write to them, and must be present on all drains so the grader can inspect them uniformly.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* `outcome_verdict` shape: `{result, findings[], invoked_at}` or `null`.\n* `prep_verdict` shape: `{result, route, reasoning}` or `null`.\n* `responder_runs` is an array of `{comment_ids[], invoked_at, result}` objects, empty by default.\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* Introducing new fields on existing schema may break deserialisation in run-log readers if they use strict parsing; a forward-compatible parse strategy must be confirmed.\n* The field shapes must be stable before M2/M3/M4 consume them — any shape change after those milestones begin is expensive.\n\n## Definition of Done\n\n* Every drain (verify or default) produces a run-log entry that includes `outcome_verdict: null`, `prep_verdict: null`, `responder_runs: []`.\n* The field shapes match the spec in `docs/initiative-multi-agent-collaboration.md`.\n* Existing run-log readers produce no errors on entries with the new fields.","projectMilestone":{"id":"a668c8fa-f026-4247-b3c5-c491c9865642","name":"2 — Record the pack's verdicts in the run log"},"priority":{"value":0,"name":"No {"id":"ABA-329","title":"`/shape:pr-prepare` skill: writes What/Why/Focus body and routes PRs to auto-merge or human review","description":"## What\n\nA new skill `/shape:pr-prepare` exists in `agent-skills-shaper` that reads stack PRs, writes a structured What/Why/Focus body on each, decides auto-merge vs human-review per the carve-out rule, and records the routing decision in `prep_verdict`.\n\n## Why\n\nThis is the PR Preparer role. {"id":"ABA-333","title":"`drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>`: one-shot command writes per-ticket grade file with `status: draft`","description":"## What\n\n`drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` produces a per-ticket grade file at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with `status: draft` and a pre-populated KR-check checklist drawn from the run-log entry for that issue.\n\n## Why\n\nWithout this command, KR grading requires the operator to read raw run-log JSON and fill out the checklist manually. The draft file is the starting point the operator confirms or corrects — separating the data-pull step from the judgement step.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* The grade file format is a markdown template with a `status:` field (values: `draft`, `confirmed`).\n* The command reads from the run-log entry for the given issue.\n* The command runs automatically on drain completion for verify-flow tickets AND can be run manually for retro-filling past tickets.\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* If the run-log entry is absent or incomplete, the command must error clearly rather than produce a misleading draft.\n\n## Definition of Done\n\n* `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` produces `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` with `status: draft` and a populated KR checklist from the run-log entry.\n* Running the command a {"id":"ABA-325","title":"`/shape:verify-implementation` skill: produces structured pass/fail verdict against ticket AC","description":"## What\n\nA new skill `/shape:verify-implementation` exists in `agent-skills-shaper` that reads the ticket body (including Task Shaper AC checklist), the diff, and the run log, and produces a structured verdict: `{result: pass|fail, findings: [], invoked_at: ...}`.\n\n## Why\n\nThis is the Outcome Verifier role — the brake that prevents silent Done on hard tickets. Without a verdict recorded in the run log, KR2 (zero silent Dones) cannot be satisfied mechanically.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* The skill has access to the git diff of the worktree and the current run-log entry.\n* A \"finding\" is a specific AC item that the diff does not satisfy, with enough detail for the operator to understand the gap.\n* The verdict schema matches the `outcome_verdict` field shape defined in the run-log schema issue.\n* If the Task Shaper output is absent, the skill falls back to the raw ticket body AC.\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* False positives (fail verdict when AC is actually met) will trigger unnecessary halts — the skill must err toward precision over recall.\n* The skill is a key correctness gate; if it can be bypassed or crashes silently, the whole initiative's value collapses.\n\n## Definition of Done\n\n* The skill exists in `agent-skills-shaper` and is registered.\n* Given a diff where all AC items are met: produces `{result: \"pass\", findings: []}`.\n* Given a diff where at least one AC item is unmet: produces `{result: \"fail\", findings: [{...}]}` with one finding per unmet AC item.\n* The verdict schema matches the `outcome_verdict` spec from the run-log schema issue.","priority":{"value":0,"name":"No {"id":"ABA-327","title":"Worker session invokes `/shape:task` before implementation on `verify`-flow tickets","description":"## What\n\nThe drain-cycle worker session for `verify`-flow tickets invokes `/shape:task` as the first step before any implementation begins, and includes the Task Shaper output in the implementer's prompt context.\n\n## Why\n\nWithout this wiring, the Task Shaper skill exists but is never invoked by the worker — the role doesn't participate in the drain flow and the AC enrichment never reaches the implementer.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* The worker session can prepend a shaping step before handing off to the implementer.\n* The Task Shaper output format is stable (the `/shape:task` skill issue is done first).\n* Non-verify-flow drains are unchanged.\n* On a §14 resume, the worker checks the Linear issue body for an existing Task Shaper output block; if found, it uses that block as context and skips re-invoking `/shape:task`. This is the mechanism that makes <issue id=\"ab81a414-a533-42b5-b545-e04e3fbc4757\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-326/ta[REDACTED_SK]\">ABA-326</issue>'s dual-write the durable artefact. See design decision §17.\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* The shaping step adds latency and token cost per ticket; if material, `limits.yml` overrides may be needed (addressed in M5).\n* If `/shape:task` surfaces irresolvable AC gaps, the skill flags them and returns (it does not hang or halt the worker). The worker passes the flagged gaps through to the implementer's context — the implementer proceeds with explicit awareness of the gaps rather than silently.\n\n## Definition of Done\n\n* A `verify`-labelled drain invokes `/shape:task` before the implementer begins.\n* The implementer's prompt context includes the Task Shaper output.\n* A non-verify-flow drain does not invoke `/shape:task`.\n* The run-log entry includes evidence that the shaping step ran.\n* A resumed verify-flow drain (§14) skips the `/shape:task` invocation if a Task Shaper output block already exists in the Linear issue body, and uses the existing block as implementer context.","priority":{"value":0,"name":"No > AGENT
```

I've now got the full picture — all 10 open issues, their descriptions, and the live status of every blocker they point at. Here's the review.

## Headline

**8 of 10 are genuinely needed. 2 are casualties of the architecture split** (ABA-330 and ABA-320) — both predate the thin-supervisor design and now overlap with the N-series that replaced that approach. Separately, the **milestone numbering is backwards relative to the real dependency order**, and there are **3 stale/missing blocker edges**.

The 10 issues fall into two layers that were authored ~2 weeks apart and never fully reconciled:

- **Legacy "verify-flow" set** (created May 28, pre-split): ABA-320, 328, 330, 334
- **New "thin-supervisor" N-series** (created Jun 10, moved in from the canceled project): ABA-370–374 (N01–N05)
- **Plus** ABA-393 (orchestrator-enforced completion, created yesterday)

## Issue-by-issue verdict

| Issue | What | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **ABA-370 N01** — contract design doc | Defines handoff schema v2 + process/workflow boundary | **Keep — this is the root.** Unblocked (ABA-361 Done). Everything else waits on it. |
| **ABA-373 N04** — supervisor records verdicts | Read side: handoff → run log | **Keep.** Only blocker is N01. |
| **ABA-372 N03** — pack emits verdicts | Write side: skills → handoff | **Keep.** Blockers all Done except N01. |
| **ABA-371 N02** — pointer-only prompt | Collapse prompt to ≤15 lines | **Keep.** Blockers all Done except N01. |
| **ABA-374 N05** — 2-cycle validation | Grades KR1 | **Keep, but stale blocker** (see below). |
| **ABA-328** — halt-on-fail | Enforcement (M3) | **Keep. Ready now** — both blockers (ABA-321, ABA-325) are Done. But collides with ABA-393 (same seam). |
| **ABA-334** — `grade --flow=verify` | Grading (M4) | **Keep.** Ready once ABA-328 lands. |
| **ABA-393** — orchestrator-enforced completion | Recovery backstop | **Keep.** But missing a blocker on ABA-328. |
| **ABA-330** — worker invokes PR Preparer; writes `prep_verdict` | M2 | **Fold/cancel.** Its mechanism (supervisor wires the worker to call pr-prepare) is exactly what N03 replaces — under thin-supervisor the *pack* emits `prep_verdict` into the handoff. This is the duplication the merge flagged. |
| **ABA-320** — `verify` label → `flow:"verify"` in run log | M1 | **Reframe or cancel.** It builds verify-label routing *into* the supervisor (`flow.py`/`labels.py`) — which N02 explicitly **rips back out** and N01's boundary chart re-decides. Building it then deleting it in N02 is wasted motion. |

## Is the order correct? No — milestone numbers invert the dependency

The milestones read 1→2→3→4→5, but the actual critical path is:

```
N01 (M5, the contract)  ──►  N03 + N04 (M2, recording)  ──►  N02 (M5)  ──►  N05 (M4)
                                  ▲
ABA-328 (M3) ── ABA-334 (M4)      │  ABA-393 (M3) shares ABA-328's seam
```

**N01 sits in milestone 5 but blocks milestone 2.** The thing numbered last is the prerequisite for almost everything. Whoever picks by milestone number will start in the wrong place. Either renumber so the contract is milestone 0/1, or just work the dependency graph and ignore the numbers.

The legacy enforcement/grading chain (328 → 334) is correctly ordered and independent of the N-series — it can proceed in parallel as soon as someone wants it.

## Blocker problems

**Stale edges (point at dead issues):**
- **ABA-320 → blocks ABA-327** — ABA-327 was *canceled today*. Dead edge.
- **ABA-374 (N05) → blockedBy ABA-368** — ABA-368 (the old skill-project validation) was *canceled* when that project closed. Remove it; N05's real blockers are N02–N04.

**Missing edge (genuine conflict, no edge):**
- **ABA-393 and ABA-328 both rewrite the same halt decision in `_drain_one_issue`.** ABA-393's own description calls ABA-328 a "MANDATORY exclusion" and says they must be designed jointly — yet there's only a `relatedTo`, no `blocks`/`blockedBy`. Whichever lands second silently rebases the other. They should be sequenced (blocker) or merged into one halt-logic ticket.

**Premature/missing edge:**
- **ABA-320 has no blocker on N01**, yet N01's boundary chart is what decides where flow routing lives. ABA-320 either needs `blockedBy N01` or should be cancelled into the N-series.

## The `flow` field — what it's for, and whether you need it

`flow` is a run-log discriminator. The `verify` **Linear label** selects it; the supervisor records `flow: "verify"` (vs `flow: null`) on the entry. Everything downstream keys off it:

- **Enforcement** (ABA-328): halt only fires when `flow == "verify" AND outcome_verdict.result == "fail"` — so ordinary drains aren't gated.
- **Grading** (ABA-334): the KR2 check and the pass-rate denominator filter on `flow == "verify"`. It's the "which drains count" selector.

So its single purpose is **making the verify pipeline opt-in per ticket** — only hard/outcome-focused tickets pay the Task-Shaper + Outcome-Verifier token and latency cost; trivial tickets drain normally.

**Is it needed? It depends on one decision you should make explicitly:**

- **If verify stays opt-in** (the original May-28 design) → **yes, `flow` is load-bearing.** It's the selector for enforcement and the filter for grading; you can't express the KRs without it.
- **If the thin-supervisor pack now always runs verify** as part of the standard entry skill (the direction N02/N03 point in) → **`flow` is largely vestigial.** When every drain verifies, `flow` is a constant, and "was this drain verified?" is better read directly from `outcome_verdict != null` than from a parallel field that can drift from it. You'd then grade all drains uniformly and drop the label.

My read: the June architecture is quietly pushing toward "the pack always verifies," which makes `flow` redundant with `outcome_verdict` presence. But the KRs and kill-condition are all written in terms of "verify-flow tickets," so dropping it isn't free — it means rewording the KRs. **This is exactly the kind of one-way-door boundary question N01 exists to settle.** I'd resolve it there rather than building ABA-320's supervisor-side resolution first.

## Recommended cleanup

1. **Cancel ABA-330**, folding its `prep_verdict` intent into N03 (write) + N04 (read).
2. **Block ABA-320 on N01** (or cancel it) — don't build supervisor-side flow routing that N02 deletes; let N01 decide whether `flow` survives at all.
3. **Remove the stale blockers**: ABA-320→327 and ABA-374→368.
4. **Add a blocker between ABA-328 and ABA-393** (or merge them) — they edit the same halt seam.
5. **Treat N01 as the next pickup** — it's unblocked and gates the entire recording + thinning chain; renumber milestones so the contract isn't labeled "5."

Want me to apply 1–4 in Linear now (the mechanical edits), and leave 5 + the `flow` keep/drop decision for you to confirm? Or hold all of it until you've digested this?

> DEVELOPER

drop the whole flow thing - correctness is the guiding principle

go ahead with your other recommendations

> AGENT

```
{"id":"ABA-393","title":"Orchestrator-enforced completion: finishing sub-agent recovers stranded committed work","description":"## What\n\nAs the drain-cycle operator running an unattended cycle, I want the orchestrator to drive a committed-but-unfinished issue to completion through a fresh finishing sub-agent before it halts, so that a weak worker that commits its slices but skips the finishing protocol no longer strands reviewable work or stops the run.\n\nThe seam lives in `_drain_one_issue` (`drain_cycle/orchestrator.py`), at the two terminal halt sites: the not-Done halt (`:781`, slug `err-issue-not-done`) and the stack-no-PRs halt (`:706`, slug `err-stack-no-prs`). Before either reverts and records, the orchestrator inspects the worktree branch. With commits beyond base (`git rev-list <base>..HEAD --count > 0`) and the issue not properly closed, it spawns a finishing-only sub-agent — reusing `worker.run_issue` with a new `prompt.build_finishing` (alongside `_stack_preamble` / `_resume_directive`) — then re-reads `linear.get_issue` and `handoff.read`. On success it joins the existing happy path (`:733`): extend the baton, record submitted PRs, tear down. On failure it falls through to the unchanged halt, now naming that recovery was attempted.\n\n**Incident that motivated this:** in cycle `793fc9ea`, <issue id=\"96cabf59-da7d-4eb7-9032-7ea75663163a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-383/n08-reference-sweep-fresh-install-check\">ABA-383</issue> ran on a `model:haiku` worker that committed 4 reviewable slices but exited without running review → `/shape:pr-finishing` → handoff → Done. The not-Done halt reverted the issue and stranded the branch.\n\n## Why\n\nCompleteness is the orchestrator's contract to enforce, not the worker's to be trusted with. The orchestrator already reads the proof signals (`final_linear_state`, `handoff.read`); the only missing move is to act on a recoverable gap instead of halting on it. The finishing sequence is mechanical and was proven by hand on <issue id=\"96cabf59-da7d-4eb7-9032-7ea75663163a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-383/n08-reference-sweep-fresh-install-check\">ABA-383</issue>, so a fixed **sonnet** sub-agent runs it reliably regardless of the issue's `model:` label, while any Critical/Required code fix — the part that needs capability — delegates to **opus**.\n\n**Rejected:** orchestrator runs `gh`/`gt` and posts the Linear comment itself — moves submission logic back into the orchestrator that AGENTS.md deliberately stripped it from. **Rejected:** trust uncommitted working-tree changes — completeness of an uncommitted tree is unknowable, so an empty or uncommitted-only branch stays a genuine failure and halts as today.\n\n## Completion\n\n* Done when: on the not-Done path, an issue left with commits beyond base and state ≠ Done triggers a sonnet finishing sub-agent; a post-spawn re-check reading Done continues the run, and still-not-Done halts with a reason naming the attempted recovery.\n* Done when: on the stack-no-PRs path, an issue Done with commits beyond base but no `pr_urls` triggers the same finishing sub-agent; a re-check reading non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton, and still-empty `pr_urls` halts.\n* Done when: an issue whose branch has no commits beyond base, or whose only changes are uncommitted, halts with the existing reason and spawns no finishing sub-agent.\n* Done when: the finishing sub-agent runs on `claude-sonnet-4-6` and its prompt instructs it to delegate Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`; at most one finishing attempt runs per issue per run; and a finishing spawn is recorded as its own run-log entry.\n\n## Tasks\n\n- [ ] **skeleton** — On the not-Done halt path in `_drain_one_issue`, detect commits beyond base, add `prompt.build_finishing`, spawn a sonnet finishing sub-agent via `worker.run_issue`, and re-check `linear.get_issue` + `handoff.read`, recovering to the happy path on Done or falling through to the existing halt (real nested `claude -p` spawn, not a stub). Done when: an issue exited not-Done with commits beyond base runs the finishing sub-agent and either reaches Done-and-continues or halts with a recovery-attempted reason, exercised end-to-end. *(Model: Opus)*\n- [ ] Extend the same recovery to the stack-no-PRs halt path (`err-stack-no-prs`): an issue Done with commits beyond base but empty `pr_urls` runs the finishing sub-agent and a re-check with non-empty `pr_urls` extends the baton. Done when: a Done-but-no-`pr_urls` issue with commits beyond base is recovered to non-empty `pr_urls` and extends the baton, or halts unchanged when recovery fails. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n- [ ] Harden and bound the recovery — run the finishing sub-agent on `claude-sonnet-4-6` with a prompt that delegates Critical/Required fixes to `claude-opus-4-7`, guard one finishing attempt per issue per run, write a run-log entry for the finishing spawn, and enrich the fall-through halt reason. Done when: a second finishing attempt within one run is refused, the finishing spawn appears as its own run-log entry, and the prompt pins sonnet with an opus fix-delegation instruction. *(Model: Sonnet)*\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* **Finishing sub-agent loops / re-strands work.** Mitigation: one-attempt-per-issue-per-run guard; `max_resume_attempts` is the cross-run backstop.\n* **Finishing sub-agent re-implements rather than finishes.** Mitigation: `build_finishing` prompt states the work is already committed and forbids re-implementation. Falsifier: a finishing run whose post-spawn `<base>..HEAD` diff adds non-review commits.\n* **\"Committed beyond base\" misfires on an ambiguous base.** Mitigation: resolve `<base>` from the same value the worker prompt used and assert it is an ancestor of HEAD before spawning.\n\n---\n\n## Relationship to this project (reconciliation — read before build)\n\nThis ticket is the **orchestrator-side backstop** for the same goal the six in-worker roles serve: completeness enforced, not trusted. It must be designed jointly with three siblings:\n\n* <issue id=\"080d520d-331a-41c8-888f-af2425d81e7a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-328/halt-on-fail-verify-flow-ticket-failing-outcome-verifier-halts-cycle\">ABA-328</issue> **(Halt-on-fail) — MANDATORY exclusion.** <issue id=\"080d520d-331a-41c8-888f-af2425d81e7a\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-328/halt-on-fail-verify-flow-ticket-failing-outcome-verifier-halts-cycle\">ABA-328</issue> makes an Outcome-Verifier FAIL *stay halted*; this ticket makes a not-Done/no-PR exit *auto-recover*. Both rewrite the same halt decision in `_drain_one_issue`. The recovery here must fire **only on \"committed-but-unfinished\", never on \"committed-but-verifier-rejected\"** — otherwise it would submit and Done a ticket the verifier deliberately rejected, breaking **KR2 (zero silent Dones)**. Detection must read the verifier verdict and skip recovery on FAIL.\n* <issue id=\"5e888b35-f824-4b90-b5d3-7b4c31c21237\" href=\"https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-330/worker-invokes-pr-preparer-after-outcome-verifier-pass-prep-verdict\">ABA-330</issue> **(PR Preparer) — KR3 alignment.** The finishing agent submits via `/shape:pr-finishing`; KR3 requires every PR carry a structured What/Why/Focus body or auto-merge route via `/shape:pr-prepare`. Decide at build whether it stays universal (a general safety net) or is gated to the verify flow to match the project's other roles.\n\nDelivery plan: `docs/delivery-plans/orchestrator-enforced-completion/` (passes `walk-delivery-plan` + `check-plan-framing`). ```json
```
```json
{
  "id": "ABA-328",
  "title": "Halt-on-fail: a ticket failing Outcome Verifier halts the cycle with worktree intact",
  "description": "## What\n\nWhen the Outcome Verifier returns a fail verdict, the drain cycle halts, the worktree is left at its current state for operator inspection, and `cycle_halt_reason` in the run log names the specific findings. This applies to **every drain** — there is no opt-in label and no flow gate.\n\n## Why\n\nWithout enforcement, the verifier's verdict is advisory — the worker can still mark Done on a fail. The halt path is what makes \"zero silent Dones\" mechanically true rather than policy-dependent. Correctness is the guiding principle, so the gate is universal: every drain runs the verifier, and any fail halts.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* The existing halt machinery (design decisions §8/§9) provides the correct extension point.\n* `cycle_halt_reason` either already exists or is added as part of this issue.\n* If the verifier crashes or times out, the halt fires by default (default-to-halt, not default-to-continue).\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* The gate condition is `outcome_verdict.result == \"fail\"`. A false-positive fail triggers an unnecessary halt, so the verifier must err toward precision over recall.\n* Leaving the worktree intact must not block subsequent drains of other tickets in the cycle.\n\n## Definition of Done\n\n* A ticket where the Outcome Verifier returns fail: the cycle halts; `cycle_halt_reason` names the failing findings; the worktree is intact.\n* A ticket where the verifier returns pass: the drain continues to Done normally.\n* `drain-cycle grade` can read the halted entry and identify it as a verifier-fail halt."
}
```

```json
{
  "id": "ABA-334",
  "title": "`drain-cycle grade`: reports pass-rate and exits nonzero on silent-Done violations",
  "description": "## What\n\n`drain-cycle grade` reads all grade files with `status: confirmed`, reports the pass-rate across the window, and exits nonzero if any entry has `final_linear_state == \"completed\"` AND `outcome_verdict == null` (a silent-Done violation). All drains are graded — there is no flow filter.\n\n## Why\n\nThis is the grading command the operator runs to assess initiative health. Without it, KR grading is manual and the silent-Done brake has no mechanical teeth.\n\n## Assumptions\n\n* Grade files with `status: draft` are excluded from the report but warned about.\n* The pass-rate denominator is the count of confirmed entries in the measurement window.\n\n## Key Risks\n\n* If the run-log entry and the grade file are out of sync (e.g. run-log updated after confirmation), the command may produce a misleading result.\n* The silent-Done detection must be exact: a Done entry with `outcome_verdict == null` is the violation; nothing else is.\n\n## Definition of Done\n\n* `drain-cycle grade` reports: total tickets graded, pass-rate, any silent-Done violations by issue ID.\n* Any silent-Done violation causes exit code ≠ 0.\n* Un-confirmed drafts are reported as warnings, not counted in the rate.\n* A run with no violations exits 0."
}
```

```json
{
  "id": "812bc9b5-1618-48b8-8041-6d6223dc6d4c",
  "description": "# Record and gate the Outcome Verifier verdict before a hard ticket lands\n\ndrain-cycle now supervises a pack of `exec:*` skills: the worker drives a Linear issue from pickup to merged PR, and the supervisor advances on the artifacts it leaves behind. One artifact it does not yet read is the proof the work is done. Under design decision §1 the worker moves its own issue to Done; the supervisor observes only process exit, never task success — so a hard ticket can land with its acceptance criteria unmet, and nothing records whether the outcome was checked.\n\nThe `exec:*` skills already produce that proof — `exec:verify` emits an Outcome Verifier verdict, `exec:pr-prepare` a PR-routing decision — but the verdicts evaporate at the layer boundary: the supervisor has nowhere to record them and no contract that reads them.\n\nThis project keeps drain-cycle's Layer-1 work only: record those verdicts, gate Done on the outcome verdict, and grade the result. Hard tickets stop shipping with missing AC, and every drained ticket's outcome becomes inspectable after the fact.\n\n> **Decision (2026-06-15): `flow` is dropped.** Correctness is the guiding principle, so the verifier, the verdict recording, and the gate apply to **every drain** — not a `verify`-labelled subset. There is no `flow` run-log field, no `verify` label as a selector, and no per-ticket opt-in. The KRs below are universal as a result.\n\n## Goal\n\nToday a ticket reaches Done on the worker's own say-so (§1), with no recorded check. This project drives that to **0 Dones without a recorded** `outcome_verdict`, and **≥ 3 of the next 5 hard tickets shipping and merging with no operator-pushed fix commits**.\n\n## What this project covers, and what it does not\n\nIn scope — drain-cycle's Layer 1 only:\n\n* The run-log schema fields the verdicts land in: `outcome_verdict`, `prep_verdict`, `responder_runs[]`, plus matching OTel span attributes.\n* The verifier-gated Done contract, which supersedes design decision §1 (the agent self-asserts Done).\n* Grading: `drain-cycle grade-draft <issue>` and `drain-cycle grade`.\n\nOut of scope — delivered elsewhere:\n\n* Authoring the `exec:*` skills — owned by \"Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills\" (done).\n* The thin-supervisor prompt re-scope and the `exec:*` cutover — milestone 5 here, merged in from \"drain-cycle supervises; the pack owns the workflow\".\n\n## Key results\n\n**KR1 — ship hard tickets without operator rescue commits** *(bet)*\n\n* Baseline: the recording and enforcement layer doesn't exist, so Done can be self-asserted unverified.\n* Target: ≥ 3 of 5 hard tickets ship and merge with no operator-pushed fix commits.\n* Measured over: the first 5 hard tickets drained after the gate lands (~2 cycles).\n* How we'll know: `drain-cycle grade-draft` auto-drafts a per-ticket grade at `~/.drain-cycle/grades/<issue>.md` for the operator to confirm; `drain-cycle grade` reports the rolled-up pass-rate.\n\n**KR2 — every Done carries a recorded verdict**\n\n* Baseline: silent self-assertion is possible today (§1); the past count is unknown because the recording fields don't exist yet.\n* Target: 0 tickets reach Done without an `outcome_verdict` in the run log.\n* Measured over: every cycle, capped at 10 cycles.\n* How we'll know: `drain-cycle grade` fails any entry where `final_linear_state == \"completed\"` **and** `outcome_verdict` is absent — exit code ≠ 0 on any violation.\n\n**KR3 — every PR is review-ready or correctly auto-merged**\n\n* Baseline: PRs from drained tickets carry whatever body the worker produced — typically a one-line subject and no review guidance.\n* Target: 0 PRs submitted without either (a) a structured What / Why / Focus body or (b) an auto-merge label with reasoning.\n* Measured over: every PR submitted, for the project's duration.\n* How we'll know: the prep step writes its decision to `prep_verdict`; `drain-cycle grade` fails any entry where `prep_verdict` is absent or claims a structured body the PR doesn't have.\n\n## Kill condition\n\nAfter the verifier runs on 5+ hard tickets across 2 cycles, kill the contract if the Outcome Verifier produces zero true-positive findings — every halt a false positive, and verifier-passed Dones no better in PR review than before. On kill: revert enforcement (milestone 3), keep the recording fields (cheap and useful), and record the negative result in `docs/design-decisions.md`.\n\n## Scope and context\n\n* **Design context:** `docs/architecture.md` (the two-layer split and the verdict-handoff seam) and `docs/design-decisions.md` §1 (the verifier-gated-Done supersession). This description is the source of truth for scope, KRs, and the kill condition."
}
```

- **ABA-334** — retitled `drain-cycle grade` (was `grade --flow=verify`); grades all drains, fails on any Done with a null verdict.

**Blockers fixed**
- **ABA-374** — removed the stale `blockedBy ABA-368` (that validation issue was canceled).
- **ABA-393** — added `blockedBy ABA-328` so the halt-on-fail logic lands first and recovery reads the verdict before acting (the mandatory FAIL-exclusion).

```
1	# Style Rules (all document types)
2	
3	Apply these during Phase 4. The goal is the shortest path to the reader's mind without losing operational facts.
4	
5	## Sentence mechanics
6	
7	**Characters as subjects, actions as verbs.** Find the doer; make it the subject; make its activity a specific verb.
8	
9	| Bad | Good |
10	|---|---|
11	| "There was a failure in the deployment process due to configuration drift." | "The deploy failed because the staging config drifted from prod." |
12	| "Request dropping occurs at high utilization." | "The load balancer drops requests above 1k RPS." |
13	| "It was decided that caching should be utilized." | "We will cache session tokens in Redis." |
14	
15	**Old before new.** Start with what the reader knows; end with the new term. The end of a sentence is the stress position — the reader emphasizes whatever sits there.
16	
17	| Bad | Good |
18	|---|---|
19	| "Edge Workers, which intercept requests at the CDN layer, will reduce load time." | "To cut load time, we will intercept requests at the CDN layer using Edge Workers." |
20	
21	**Hand-product test.** Every task, goal, or milestone must imply a tangible end product the reader can visualize. "Investigate latency" fails; "a report naming the three slowest queries in /orders" passes.
22	
23	## Nominalizations — reverse them
24	
25	Abstract nouns derived from verbs freeze action. Turn them back into verbs.
26	
27	| Frozen | Active |
28	|---|---|
29	| utilization | use |
30	| implementation | implement |
31	| optimization | optimize |
32	| investigation | investigate |
33	| facilitation | help / enable |
34	| determination | decide |
35	| reduction | reduce / cut |
36	
37	## Vocabulary watchlist
38	
39	Sweep the document for these and replace:
40	
41	| Avoid | Prefer | Why |
42	|---|---|---|
43	| load-bearing / vital | essential / required | Overworked metaphor distracts from the technical reality |
44	| bottleneck | shortage / delay / hold-up | Cliché that hides the specific cause of constriction |
45	| target | objective / goal / aim | Often "attained" without being "hit" |
46	| blueprint | plan / scheme / spec | Implies a final design; usually misused for early ideas |
47	| ceiling | limit / maximum | "Raise the limit" is clearer than "raise the ceiling" |
48	| interface with | talk to | Jargon for a plain act |
49	| impact (as verb) | affect | "Impact" blunts a painful truth |
50	| leverage (as verb) | use | Same |
51	| basically / actually / quite / virtually / a bit | *(delete)* | Little qualifiers dilute authority |
52	| "at this point in time" | now | Shortest path to the mind |
53	| "in order to" | to | Same |
54	| "It should be noted that…" / "It is important to…" | *(start directly)* | Throat-clearing adds nothing |
55	| full and complete / each and every | full / each | Redundant pairs |
56	
57	## Keep the exact term; cut only the hiding metaphor
58	
59	Use the precise technical term whenever it is accurate — `mutex`, `P99`, `idempotent` earn their place. Replace a metaphor only when it hides the cause the reader needs. A figure of speech that names no mechanism leaves the reader guessing; the concrete noun states it.
60	
61	| Hides the cause | States it |
62	|---|---|
63	| "The seam lives in the orchestrator." | "The recovery points are in the orchestrator." |
64	| "The missing move is the status check." | "The orchestrator checks the status." |
65	| "This keeps reviewable work from being stranded." | "This prevents stranded branches." |
66	
67	## The pruning safety rule
68	
69	Concision serves clarity, never the reverse. Before deleting a sentence, ask: does it carry a dependency, risk, assumption, or constraint? If yes, compress it into fewer words — do not remove it. A short document that omits the rollback plan is worse than a longer one that keeps it.
70	
```

```
1	# Plans, Goals, OKRs, and Milestones
2	
3	## Goals: justify the project by the gap
4	
5	A goal exists to close the gap between the current state and the success state. State both, with numbers.
6	
7	| Bad | Good |
8	|---|---|
9	| "The goal is to improve the efficiency of our API and utilize better caching." | "Our API serves 1k RPS today. Q3 traffic forecasts 10k RPS. We will re-architect the load balancer to sustain 15k RPS." |
10	
11	Note what the good version does: noncontroversial fact → disturbance → quantified success state, in three short sentences, with no framework labels.
12	
13	## OKRs: a specific result that eliminates a palpable cost
14	
15	Each key result names the current value, the success value, and (where useful) the mechanism.
16	
17	| Bad | Good |
18	|---|---|
19	| "Significantly improve dashboard speed." | "Reduce P95 dashboard load time from 4.5s to under 800ms using Edge Workers." |
20	| "Increase reliability." | "Cut weekly pager incidents from 12 to under 3 by adding circuit breakers to the payments service." |
21	
22	If a key result has no number, it is a wish, not a result. If it has no cost attached (what the current state is costing in money, time, or pain), the reader has no reason to care — state the cost in the surrounding prose.
23	
24	## Milestones: achievements, not labels
25	
26	Group work into containers that state a **summary effect** — what was accomplished, not where you were on the calendar.
27	
28	| Bad | Good |
29	|---|---|
30	| "1. Frontend; 2. Data" | "1. Built responsive React components; 2. Migrated user records to a sharded Postgres cluster" |
31	| "Phase 1: Research" | "Identified the three highest-cost queries blocking checkout" |
32	
33	**MECE check for milestone groups.** Groups must not overlap and must cover the whole project.
34	
35	| Bad (overlapping) | Good (exclusive) |
36	|---|---|

54	- Does every key result carry a number, and does the surrounding prose name the cost of the status quo?
55	- Are the milestones achievement headings (a result), not calendar or category labels?

57	- Did risks, assumptions, and dependencies survive pruning?
```

> AGENT

Standard formed. Reading the milestone-4 draft against it, the mismatch is clear — and it's not subtle. **Verdict: reject** (two load-bearing failures), and there's a factual bug your renumber introduced.

## Findings

**1. The opening is provenance, not an achievement — `reject`-level.**
> "Serves the vendor-agnostic supervisor bet (was KR1/KR3 of the merged "drain-cycle supervises; the pack owns the workflow" project)."

Your other three milestones open with the *accomplished state* — "Every run-log entry carries…", "A ticket reaches Done only with…", "`drain-cycle grade-draft` auto-drafts…". This one opens with lineage and a parenthetical changelog. Breaks point-first / old-before-new: the reader meets where-it-came-from before what-it-does.

**2. No "Done when:" clause — `reject`-level.**
The sibling milestones each close with a verifiable "Done when:". This one has none, so it states no visualizable end product (Phase 5, plan-doc check). That's the format inconsistency you felt.

**3. Metaphor hides the mechanism — note.**
> "the supervisor sheds workflow prose"

`style-rules.md`: keep the term, cut the hiding metaphor → "the worker prompt drops its inlined procedure."

**4. Stale cross-references after your renumber — factual bug.**
> "(N03/N04, milestone 2)" … "(N05, milestone 4)"

Post-renumber, verdict-recording (N03/N04) is **milestone 1**, and N05 grades under **milestone 3**. Both numbers are now wrong. The fix is to reference the N-nodes and *not* hard-code a sibling's number — those numbers will drift again.

**5. Dated changelog appendix — fold in.**
> "Note (2026-06-15): `flow` is dropped…"

It carries a real constraint (schema v2 omits `flow`; N02 deletes `flow.py`), so don't cut it — fold it into the prose, matching the siblings which carry no dated notes.

## Rewrite (applied)

I matched the sibling format — point-first achievement, then a "Done when:", with the `flow` constraint folded in and the volatile milestone-numbers replaced by N-node references:
